ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u8	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Mr. Speaker! As far as the measures that have been taken to increase the innovation of the Polish economy are concerned, I would like to stress at the outset that, of course, the increase in R &amp; D expenditure in Poland is one of the strategic objectives of the government of the Republic of Poland. In this context, it will be crucial both to increase public R &amp; D expenditure and to stimulate more effectively the growth of private spending for R &amp; D. At the outset, I would like to point out that the Innovation Council has been set up. This board consists of representatives of individual departments, both development and finance, culture and national heritage, science and higher education, health, education, digitalisation and treasure. This Gremium, please, initiates actions and proposes solutions that will actively support entrepreneurs in the search for new products and processes and involve Polish capital and consequently contribute to creating competitive advantages for our economy. The task of the Council is also - but it seems to me, first of all, to coordinate the instruments of innovation policy and to develop a system of incentives for entrepreneurs and representatives of the world of science, which are intended to contribute to the increase in R &amp; D expenditure. At this point, I would also like to point out that the completion of this active impact on R &amp; D policy will also be the concreteisation of the Council's efforts to modernise or remodel public procurement, which should take into account attractive solutions, products and processes based on the instruments introduced. Another area which is of particular interest to the Ministry of Development is, of course, the national intelligent specialisations that have been identified for the nearest perspective. These are economic priorities in the area of R &amp; D and innovation in order to concreteise investments in areas that ensure the added value of the economy and its competitiveness in foreign markets. This process, the process of identifying these smart specialisations, was also to involve socio-economic and scientific partners in order to enable them to discover those areas where the country has the chance to stand out internationally. Today it is a list of 20 national smart specialisations that have been grouped into five thematic departments. The first department is a healthy society, the second department is the agri-food, forest-tree and environmental bioeconomy, the third department is sustainable energy, while the fourth department is natural raw materials and waste management, the fifth, the most horizontal, these are innovative technologies and industrial processes. In addition, and I would like to point out very strongly, most of the activities envisaged in the operational programme "Smart Development" for the period 2014-2020 have been launched recently. We have also increased the forecast of spending, which was for individual years, including 2016, which, please, is a real support for entrepreneurs in R &amp; D. The most important instruments that we intend to launch under this programme are primarily activities that finance R &amp; D by companies, so-called. The rapid path or the Demonstrator, also sectoral R &amp; D programmes, support instruments using investment and venture capital funds, the creation or development of this R &amp; D infrastructure - in particular we want R &amp; D centres to be equipped with - support, as well as the implementation of R &amp; D results by micro, small and medium-sized enterprises, as well as the financing of innovative costs of services provided on this occasion to micro, small and medium-sized enterprises by business environment institutions, as well as the financing of industrial property protection costs. Moreover, this year we will launch a special loan for technological innovation as well as a guarantee fund for micro, small and medium-sized enterprises. So that the wide range of actions still this year is to be launched. In fact, we are planning to launch 23 competitions under the 15 instruments of the Operational Programme Smart Development. In this way, competitions will be announced within the framework of all the activities of this programme which were planned to be announced. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u23	Thank you. Please respond to Minister Zbigniew Babalski.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u24	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! To answer your question, I would like to confirm that the draft law on the suspension of the sale of agricultural property of the Treasury and on the amendment of certain other laws are currently underway. This project was addressed to inter-ministerial arrangements and public consultation on 8 January 2016 and it is therefore not true that there was no public consultation. On 29 January 2016, an inter-ministerial agreement conference took place. On 9 February 2016, and therefore yesterday, this project was addressed to the Permanent Committee of the Council of Ministers. According to the WW. For a period of 5 years, the sale of the property of the Agricultural Property Resource of the State Treasury will be suspended. It is also proposed to introduce a number of changes, including: in the Act on the Shaping of the Agricultural System of 11 April 2003 and the Act of 19 October 1991 on the Management of Agricultural Real Estate of the State Treasury. Pursuant to the draft law, the law you mentioned on 5 August 2015 concerning the formation of the agricultural system is repealed. All the proposed amendments, I would like to make this clear, are intended to implement the principle of the Article. 23 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland, according to which this principle of the agricultural system in Poland is based on family farms. At the same time, the proposed amendments do not affect any other constitutional rights and guarantees. Mr President, similar solutions, perhaps more restrictive, also exist in the countries of the European Union. As we discuss this law, I will give concrete examples. Now about the art. 4, about the inheritance. The draft law on the suspension of the sale of agricultural property of the Treasury and on the amendment of certain other laws does not provide for regulations which could be considered as an infringement of the right of inheritance. There is no nationalisation in the proposed law, Mr President. The provisions of this law only provide that the Agricultural Property Agency will be entitled, I repeat, to rights which it already has, on the basis of the Act on the formation of the agricultural system in force since 2003, i.e. the right of first purchase and the right to acquire agricultural property. The condition provided for in the draft law that the purchaser of agricultural real estate may be an individual farmer alone will not apply to the acquisition of real estate as a result of inheritance and recovery. In view of the above, the right of inheritance is in no way restricted. Moreover, it will not apply to persons who have acquired property as a result of inheritance or recovery record to the holding on the property acquired for at least 10 years. It's completely excluded from this. By extending the answer to this question - in turn, provided for in Article. 7 point 7 of the proposed law changes concerning the exercise by the Agricultural Property Agency of the right to acquire agricultural property will apply only to a situation in which the acquisition takes place on a basis other than the sale contract. And actually here. This may be an inheritance situation, but this other situation is a court ruling, a registered certificate of inheritance. Here I want to explain that it could be, for example. handing over the land in the form of a will. But I want to explain clearly, here you don't have to be a farmer, you don't have to have agricultural rights. I want to make it clear that the agency has powers, not obligations, which means it can benefit or not. This doesn't apply in the first... [[But...]] Mr President, listen, this does not apply to the first line of succession, I repeat, and this is a hard line in the proposed law. Once again, in such a situation, the agency, acting in favour of the State Treasury, will be able to make statements on the acquisition of this property for payment of the equivalent monetary value. Of course, the question cannot be overlooked that the option provided for in this provision is an entitlement, not an obligation of the agency. The Agency is expected to use this option only to prevent speculative rotation of agricultural real estate. The second point that you raised, Mr President, concerns Article. 21, right? [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u31	Thank you very much. Please respond to Minister Zbigniew Babalski.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u32	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! The main irregularities that occurred in the West Pomeranian Voivodeship concerned the sale of real estate from the Agricultural Property Resources of the State Treasury. In particular, they relied on the participation of limited persons who met the criteria of an individual farmer, but who could actually act in favour of third parties. On 17 November 2015, the Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development instructed the then President of the Agricultural Property Agency to halt all activities resulting from the tasks of the Agricultural Property Agency - this Minister, I will remind you, was and is now Minister Krzysztof Jurgiel - in particular concerning the opening and running of tenders. The basic form of real estate development of the Agricultural Property Resource of the State Treasury is therefore currently leased. The regulations contained in the draft law on the suspension of the sale of agricultural property of the Treasury and on the amendment of certain laws aim at strengthening the protection of agricultural land in Poland against speculative buy-out by domestic and foreign persons, and should therefore prevent irregularities in the management of real estate of the stock. With regard to the amendments to the Law of 19 October 1991 on the Management of Agricultural Property of the Treasury, it is foreseen that after the expiry of a 5-year period, the sale of all agricultural real estate of the Agricultural Property Resources of the State Treasury will first be disposed of by a limited tender, which will take account in particular of the provisions of the Act which will determine who will first be able to acquire the land. The purpose of the proposed regulation is to support family farms. The draft law also includes solutions adopted after the protests by farmers in the West Pomeranian Voivodeship. These solutions consist in introducing financial sanctions for failure by the purchaser to comply with the undertaking submitted in the contract for 10 years, e.g. the personal pursuit of agricultural activities on land acquired from the stock, and prohibit the mortgage on immovable property acquired from the stock without the approval of the Agricultural Property Agency. In addition, in line with the recommendations of the Supreme Audit Chamber, the draft law sets out cases in which the Agricultural Property Agency will be able to agree to the lease agreement. It should be made clear that the solutions contained in the proposed law will make it difficult or even end the fictitious transaction process on the so-called. poles, because it will not be possible to acquire real estate from so-called. a pole by a person who is not a farmer and who does not intend to carry out agricultural activities on purchased land. Action to - as you are asking in detail - together with a group of Members, the elimination of emerging irregularities has also been taken by the President of the Agricultural Property Agency. As provided by the President of the Agency: Firstly, all work on the elimination of irregularities will be carried out in conjunction with the social councils appointed in recent weeks by the director of the field departments and with the participation of representatives of the provincial agricultural chambers. Secondly, the field managers, including the newly appointed director of the Field Branch in Szczecin of the Agricultural Property Agency, were obliged to respond on an ongoing basis to emerging signals and doubts as to the impartiality of the members of the tender committees and to analyse them. Thirdly, in the event of a malfunction of the tender, directors are to inform the President of the Agency and the competent authorities without delay. I mean the police or the D.A.'s office. Fourthly, in the case of transactions the method of financing of which may give rise to reasonable doubt will be addressed to the General Financial Information Inspector. Fifthly, the organisational units of the Office of the President of the Agricultural Property Agency shall carry out on an ongoing basis checks and investigations resulting from complaints and the applicants shall be informed of the results of these proceedings and the activities of the Agricultural Property Agency in this respect. Following final inspection procedures, appropriate conclusions and recommendations shall be addressed to the director of the field departments. Sixthly, all employees of the Agricultural Property Agency - I repeat: all - have been obliged to make declarations on possible cases of acquisition of agricultural property of the Treasury both by them and by members of their families. Seventhly, measures have been taken to amend the working rules on the obligation of all workers to present their statements on an ongoing basis in order to prevent a conflict of interest. ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u71	Thank you. Please take the floor of Minister Paweł Salek. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u72	Mr. Marshal! Dear Members, Decision of the European Parliament of 6 October establishing the so-called "European Parliament'. The stabilisation reserve mechanism introduced a mechanism for controlling the allowance market into the greenhouse gas emissions trading scheme. This mechanism could and can function at the time of the large excess market allowances obtained from the transfer of emission allowances from the auction pool. This mechanism can also function in the event of a shortage of market power. The current government has decided to bring an action before the Court of Justice of the European Union. This proposal states that we are calling for the annulment of the Council and Parliament's decision of 6 October, because, in our opinion, the government's reserve created by limiting the pool of allowances available on the market will lead to an increase in the price of allowances, so that companies in Poland will be forced to change the fuel used to less emissions, thus influencing the energy mix at both the level of companies and the country as a whole. The government, Poland does not recognise this way of creating a market and influencing the carbon market, CO, because it is related to far-reaching dependence on raw materials that are not available in our energy and fuel structure. In general, the government recognises that further reductions in emissions in the energy sector would be some discrimination against national energy structures and would be unreasonable in an efficient way. It would thus reduce Poland's competitiveness at the level of the national economy, and would not safeguard the sovereignty of the country in terms of shaping its own fuel mix. The complaint was lodged at the beginning of January 2016. The preparatory process concerning the lodging of the complaint has been in principle since the start of the appointment of the new government, i.e. since the turn of November and December 2015. All documents and justifications have been forwarded to the court and at this point we are waiting for the official registration of the complaint, as well as information on the exchange of correspondence between the parties, which will serve to establish or rather show the positions of both parties. As regards time-related matters, with what it will look like, we estimate that about 6 weeks after registration in the Official Journal of the European Union, the notice that such a complaint is being made will be published. We estimate that there will be countries such as Bulgaria, Romania, Croatia, the Czech Republic and Hungary that will join us in the judicial process. Thank you very much.

PLN for the shares of both mines, and the offer of the company Społem Plus amounted to 263 531 thousand. At the same time, the offer of Spolem Plus was rejected because the investor did not provide reliable sources of financing for the transaction. Therefore, only the PAK Power Plant has been negotiated. With the power plant team, negotiations were held in April 2012, from March to April 2012, and in the course of these negotiations, it is worth noting that three offers were made. The first offer, preliminary, before the start, was almost 150 million. The binding offer was presented at an amount of almost 121 million. During the negotiations there was an improved offer binding for almost 185 million, and the final price negotiated amounted to PLN 175.5 million, i.e. this price discrepancy at the very beginning of this process was quite large. The negotiations, which ended on 19 April, did not cover labour issues. These are generally agreed between the trade union and the investor, in which case they have failed and no agreement has been reached with the social side. The plant team committed to an investment of 250 million and declared workers' guarantees to Adamowa and Konin, directly employed in production, with the extraction of coal for 36 months, and to other workers for a period of 6 months. The price that was set was 6,8% higher than the lower threshold of the value recommended by the advisor, by 18.3% lower than the upper threshold of the value recommended by the privatisation advisor but by more than 70% lower than the upper threshold of the value derived from the valuation using the comparative method. Because it is important to remember that at least two offers are accepted for privatisation. In this case, the valuations proposed by the advisor were two, i.e. the discounted cash flow method and the multiplier method. And here there are very large discrepancies, because with the accepted offer, i.e. the discounted cash flow method, the price per share ranged between PLN 6,18 and PLN 8,08, while if a multiplier method were to be accepted, this price should be in the range of PLN 18,22 per share to PLN 22,48 per share of the company. The advisor recommended a lower price because, as he claimed, the time horizon of the mine's operations is short. It was expected that mining would take place there by 2017. However, NIK - and we agree with this view - notes that despite the assumption of the end of the extraction, it was assumed that investment expenditure would increase in the following years and that their total level over the forecast period would be 241 million only in the Adamów mine, while the total investment commitment of 250 million was related to two mines. That was supposed to lower the price, too. Therefore, the final price proposed was 67 million for the Adamów mine and estimated [[♪ The bell ♪]] If we accept the second method rejected by the counselor, it should be 177 million, only for the Adams. And the final price for both mines is 175 million. We believe that this privatisation procedure was indeed flawed, all the more so that the operation of the mine was profitable, the more that the operating resources at its disposal amounted to PLN 65 million and, what is important, the mine was not in debt. So we view this privatisation process as very bad. We are now examining and analysing the implementation of investment commitments and contractual obligations and we will have an answer on this matter within approximately 1.5 months. First of all, we are looking at the issue of investment.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u88	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! In response to the question concerning the development of the oil and gas industry in Podkarpacie, I kindly inform you that Podkarpacie - which is probably well known - has been an important area associated with the extraction of hydrocarbons in Poland for many, many years. The first mines were built in this area already in the 19th century. Since then, to this day, natural gas and oil mining activities in the area have been carried out. The operation of hydrocarbons in Podkarpacie runs the PGNiG Division in Sanok. The extraction of high methane and nitrogened natural gas and crude oil takes place in 36 mines: 18 gas, 13 gas and 5 oil mines. Currently, works are being carried out on 88 natural gas deposits and 40 oil deposits. On 85 natural gas deposits permanent exploitation is carried out, while other gas deposits are in preparation for development. On 36 oil deposits, continuous exploitation is carried out, while the remaining four deposits are in preparation for the start of extraction. The extraction takes place in this area with approximately 2 thousand. oil and gas wells with different depths of production horizons available. In the Carpathian area the annual extraction of raw materials is set at 1.5 billion m3. In 2015, six wells on deposits already operated in Sanoku were put into operation: Przemyśl 284 and Przemyśl 282K, Dziekków 23, Ksiżpol 19, Zalesie 2 and Zalesie 21, one well of Dąbrowa 1K Buda under test operation, and two new deposits were put into operation: the Białoboki Bia3obki bed deposit. The total increase in mining capacity amounted to about 7 thousand last year. m3 / h calculated on the methane gas. These data show the degree of involvement of entities with the involvement of the State Treasury in the development of the oil and gas industry in this section. In this context, it should be noted that in previous years, intensive seismic and drilling research has been and is being carried out in the area of the Podkarpackie Voivodeship, which aims to discover new oil and gas deposits. In the area of Sanoka, Krosna and Jasła, as well as Rzeszów, seismic photos of 2D and 3D are taken, on the basis of which further drilling is designed. At the same time, seismic research is being carried out in the area of the city of Przemyśl. The result of the work is the discovery in Podkarpackie Voivodeship of new natural gas deposits in Siedleczki, Przeworska and Kramarzówka region. Two deep drilling sites near Sanok - Dukla 1, and Przemyśl - Kramarzówka 1, have discovered forward-looking levels with great opportunities for commercial oil and natural gas flows. Drilling and exploration will continue in these areas. Further boreholes are expected to be carried out in order to obtain new deposits, to identify the deposits already discovered and to maintain production on exploited deposits. One of the main operating facilities, on which there is still a large resource and production potential, is the natural gas deposit in the vicinity of Przemyśl. In the opinion of the PGNiG Capital Group, the area of Podkarpackie Voivodeship is still one of the most forward-looking areas for exploration of oil and natural gas in Poland. At the same time, it should be noted that in the Podkarpackie Voivodeship there are underground natural gas warehouses in the towns of Brzeźnica, Husów, Strachocina and Swarzów. They play an important role in ensuring energy security through the ability to maintain mandatory stocks of natural gas and commercial quantities and increase our country's energy independence during peak natural gas demand. Currently, an investment in expansion of the first of the above mentioned is carried out. warehouses, i.e. Underground Earth Gas Warehouse Brzeźnica. It should be noted at the same time that the main objective of the PGNiG Capital Group is to maintain a stable extraction of natural gas and oil from domestic deposits. The PGNiG Capital Group will continue its exploration and exploration of natural gas and oil deposits in Poland on its most forward-looking concessions. At the same time, I would like to inform the High House that PGNiG's bodies are undergoing dynamic changes. Before the newly appointed board, which will start functioning, I hope, from tomorrow, will be the permanent task of ensuring the company's proper functioning and development also in the area of Podkarpacie, and perhaps mainly in the area of Podkarpacie. [[♪ The bell ♪]] One of the first tasks of the new management board of the company will be to verify the restructuring activities that have taken place in recent years in order to determine their purpose and rationality from the point of view of the interest and strategy of the State and the interests of the company. All decisions will be assessed and corrective actions will be taken into account in the company's new strategy. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u138	We proceed with the examination of item 11 of the agenda: Report from the Committee on Social Policy and the Family on the Government's draft law on State aid to raise children (prints No 216 and 238). ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u139	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present to the High House the report of the committee on the Government's draft law on State aid to raise children contained in print No 238. The first reading of the project took place on 9 February 2016 at the 11th meeting of the Sejm. The Sejm pursuant to Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft to the committee for consideration. On 9 February, the Social Policy and Family Committee examined the Government's draft law on State aid to raise children, print No 216, and adopted a report on it. The proposed law introduces new benefits, educational benefits into the legal system. This benefit will be granted to citizens of the Republic of Poland and to other persons who, under Community rules and international agreements, have been granted similar rights to those held by citizens in respect of social benefits. At the same time, the law conditiones, makes the right to provide educational services subject to residence in the territory of the Republic of Poland for the period during which the benefit is to be granted. Education for every entitled child will be entitled to 500 PLN per month. The benefit for the second and every subsequent child will be granted regardless of the income the family earns, while the benefit for the first child will be granted if the income of the family does not exceed 800 PLN per person in the family, and if the disabled child is in the family, the amount of 1200 PLN. The witness will continue until the child is 18 years old. The implementation of the educational benefit will be part of the commune. In the course of the examination of the project, the Commission adopted inter alia. an amendment assuming that the child is also a member of the family of both parents where, in accordance with the judgment of the court, the child remains in the custody of the two parents divorced, separated or separated. Another amendment sets out how to calculate the benefit for an incomplete month in the event of the child being cared for alternately between the two parents. Another amendment allows applications for payment of the benefit by electronic means through the electronic system provided by the Social Insurance Institution and by national banks providing electronic services. Another amendment assumes that the benefit will be shared when the child is born or is 18 years old during a given month. Another assumes that the Council of Ministers will be able to increase the amount of benefits and the criteria for entitlement to them. 15 minority proposals have been submitted concerning, inter alia, clarification of the title of the law, i.e. on State aid for raising certain children, granting a child's parental benefit of PLN 500 per month per child in the family irrespective of the income of the family, enabling parents to receive benefits for a younger child when they are over 18 years of age, introducing a zloty rule for gold in the case of persons slightly above the income thresholds entitlement to benefits for one child - if the income threshold was exceeded, the benefit would not be deducted but reduced by the amount by which the criterion was exceeded - non-compliance with the income criteria for single parents, the introduction of the upper income criterion - would not benefit if the income per person in the family exceeds PLN 2500 - the statement that the education proceedings are carried out by the Social Insurance Institution. Ladies and gentlemen, these are the most important amendments that were tabled to the government project during the committee's work. I encourage and ask the High House to adopt the attached report in print No 238. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u308	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The proposed bill amending the constitution is, in our opinion, the only way out of the constitutional crisis we have right now. This proposal includes five elements. The first element is the selection of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal by qualified majority. Two-thirds of the votes. This is the majority needed to amend the Constitution, and at the same time the majority that makes all political environments have to agree to the election of the members of the Constitutional Tribunal. This is so important that we deprive these patches of belonging to the parties, political backgrounds of judges and indeed the Constitutional Tribunal becomes a supra-political, trans-party body, it will actually have the important role played by the constitutional judiciary in every democratic state. Another element is the transfer of the cognition from the Constitutional Tribunal to the Supreme Court to examine the compatibility of the Constitutional Tribunal Act with the Constitution and ratified international agreements. We do not want, ladies and gentlemen, the situation we had last December when the Constitutional Tribunal became a judge in its own case. It is in this exceptional case that the Supreme Court should decide whether the Constitutional Tribunal Act is compatible with the Constitution and ratified international agreements. Another element, very important from the point of view of this crisis caused by the election of three judges by the Sejm of the previous term of office, as well as by the Sejm of the current term of office, of those three judges whose President Rzepliński does not wish to allow for a ruling, which the President has sworn, namely we propose to increase the personal composition of the Constitutional Tribunal from 15 to 18 judges. Another element is the appointment of the President and Vice-President of the Constitutional Tribunal by the President from among the three candidates presented by the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. And the last element that brings together the whole of this change, the change that ends this constitutional crisis and at the same time protects the Polish state from the possibility of repeating such a situation in the future, is the end of the term of office of all judges of the Constitutional Tribunal within 60 days of the entry into force of the proposed constitutional novel. Ladies and gentlemen, what are the reasons? First of all, the situation we have right now can only be compared to the Gordian node, but in this situation we can call it the double Gordian node. The situation cannot be resolved on the basis of an amendment of the Constitutional Tribunal Act or any other legal action at the statutory level. The only solution to this situation is a constitutional novel. Of course, this problem can be resolved on political grounds, and the High House does not depend on the will of President Rzepliński, we are not able to force the President of the Constitutional Tribunal to include three judges in the formation of the court, nor are we able to force President Andrzej Duda to take the oath of three judges elected by the Sejm of the previous term of office, and that is a simple explanation: because, according to the Constitution, the Constitutional Tribunal has 15 members, and at the moment 15 judges of the tribunal have taken the oath, and if President Andrzej Duda had taken the oath of three judges elected by the Sejm of the previous term, he would have broken the constitution, and the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal would then have 18, and in no way this constitutional crisis would have been resolved. Ladies and gentlemen, this is actually a delicate, minor amendment to the basic law. In many speeches, ladies and gentlemen, there are voices that the constitution is a sacred thing. I disagree with that argument. The Constitution is the legal foundation of the Polish state, but the Holy Scripture is a sacred writing and it is not subject to change. [[Applause]] Let us remember that the Constitution of the Republic of Poland of 1997 has already been amended twice and nothing really prevents this constitutional crisis from being resolved precisely by amending the Constitutional Tribunal rules. As for the comments that appear on this proposal, I would like to propose, if we go to second reading, if it is addressed to the Legislative Committee, I presume, this draft law amending the Constitution, introducing a mechanism to safeguard the choice of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, because this is the most common argument against this proposal, namely that the High House will not collect 2/3 of the votes needed to select judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. Our proposal is as follows: if the Sejm does not select by qualified majority within the prescribed time limit 2/3 of the votes will then be elected from among the candidates submitted by the Sejm by the National Judicial Council, as is the case in the case of the general court. This is a fuse to ensure the stability and security of the formation of the ruling Constitutional Tribunal. We do not want to repeat the situation that is taking place in many Western European countries, where sometimes 28 times a vote is taken on the election of a member of the constitutional court. Another element is the issue of composition. There are voices that 18 is an even number, and it would be worth it to have an odd membership. Of course, such a proposal is as much to consider, the members of the Constitutional Tribunal can also be 19, and 21. In our view, this number should be considerably higher than 15, due to the number of cases affecting the Constitutional Court and the number of judges needed to rule on these cases. Another thing, ladies and gentlemen, is to ensure the safety of those judges who are now judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, and at the time of the entry into force of this novel, namely the end of the term of office of all judges, to ensure that they are retiring. This is an important thing, which must also be ensured at the second reading stage, so that the resting state is automatically assigned to those judges whose term of office will be interrupted. We don't want to harm the judges in any way. Ladies and gentlemen, moving to conclusions: we are primarily committed to ending this constitutional crisis as soon as possible. And our solution is actually the only substantive solution to this crisis. [[Applause]] There's no other way out than a constitutional novel. There's no other means, and that's what we need to be clear about. If any of you know another legal measure to resolve the constitutional crisis, I will be very pleased to get acquainted with it, but I have not yet heard, although I have been very interested in this subject, unfortunately I have not heard from the Members of the High House how this constitutional crisis could be resolved. An important element in the discussion on the resolution of the constitutional crisis was the previous meeting with representatives of the Venice Commission, where we heard from the president of the Venice Commission that the possible solution to the constitutional crisis was the amendment of the Constitution. And that's a very important argument. [[Applause]] Ladies and gentlemen, we want a calm, substantive discussion about our proposal to get out of the constitutional crisis. In order to resolve this crisis, the agreement of the High House is needed, the agreement of all political groups is needed, so we encourage you to sit down together and think about how to amend the Constitution in order to end this constitutional crisis. We do not care about digging up whose fault it is, who did it first, who broke the standards, who did the first harm to the Polish state, spreading this constitutional crisis. We want this crisis to end. And if you care about that, we ask you very much to take a calm, factual, substantive approach to this problem, to this issue. We want to solve this crisis together. Let us show the public that Parliament, if it has the will to solve an important social problem, is able to mobilise itself. The end of the constitutional crisis is, in our view, a priority for the High House at the moment. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u309	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear ten-minute statements on behalf of the clubs. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Marek Ast, Law and Justice have the floor. [[Resumement request.]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u310	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the draft parliamentary law amending the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, print No 166. The current constitution has been in force since 1997, it is worth recalling that it has been nearly 20 years. It was actually adopted on a broad consensus basis, of course, but by a parliament that was then dominated by left-wing environments. I think that after 20 years of the Constitution, there is indeed a time to look at constitutional regulations, how they work. It is certainly necessary to have a broad discussion about a possible revision of the Constitution, and at the beginning of the term of office, Marshal Morawiecki spoke about it. We, too, as an environment of law and justice, have talked many times about the need for changes to the constitution. On the other hand, this proposal of the Kukiz15 movement concerns the specific situation and the specific rules contained in the Constitution and refers to the Constitutional Tribunal. Indeed, it is worth recalling the reasons for this initiative. It is worth recalling, because such wordings fall as if we had a constitutional crisis. These are big words, there is no such thing in Poland. KRS judges criticize, among others. the provision transferring to the powers of the Supreme Court the ruling on constitutional compliance and the ratified international agreements, the ratification of which required the prior consent of the law, of the provisions issued pursuant to Article. 197 constitutions. It can create multiple perturbations. I will also refer for the second time to the opinion of the national judicial council, which has not left the dry thread on the record of the terms of office of all the current judges of the Constitutional Tribunal at the end of the 60th day following the date of entry into force of the proposed law, i.e. before the end of their term of office. This, in the view of the National Courts of Justice, infringes the lex retro non agit principle, according to which the law should not have legal effect in relation to the facts arising before its entry into force. Moreover, it is not simply a violation of the principle of non-retroactivity, but a violation of the constitutional guarantee of independence of the judge of the Tribunal consisting of his irrevocability during the term of office laid down directly in the Constitution. In a legal state, it is not permissible to amend the Constitution merely to resolve the current political dispute as it would imply instrumental treatment of the fundamental law. ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u408	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform Club on the opinion on the government draft law amending the Family and Care Code Act. However, not all servants interpret this provision in this way. There are situations where courts rule differently. And while there are few situations like this, there should not be any case in which a family has been separated because of poverty. More separation is threatened by families defined, often too hastily, as helpless families in caring and householding matters. According to the explanatory memorandum submitted by the applicants, this may apply, on the basis of data from the Central Statistical Office, approx. 227,000. families. The statement that the family is helpless in caring and upbringing is a statement of a very general and subjective interpretation of the law by the relevant services. Putting a child in foster care is a failure of family support activities by people who pay for specific actions. These people should also take some form of responsibility for their assignment. Of course, there are cases where you absolutely need to take a child from a natural family, but these should be emergency situations where the child's health or life is at risk. In any other case, society and the state, through their institutions, should not allow this to happen. Therefore, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club supports the proposal to amend the Family and Care Code Act as proposed by the applicant, recognising the fact that the clarification of the rules will eliminate social pathology, which we regard as placing children in foster care against the will of parents because of poverty. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u418	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club, I am pleased to present an opinion on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Family and Care Code Act, print No 164 and 210. Ladies and gentlemen, it should be pointed out at the outset that the construction of the standard in the form referred to in prints 164 and 210 leaves much to be desired. In the first place, I pay attention to the expression in particular, which was used in the second sentence of the paragraph. 1. In fact, the catalogue of reasons considered sufficient by the legislator and related to the welfare of the child, under the conditions of the first sentence of that paragraph, will result in the taking away of the child and placing him in one of the forms of foster care. Continuing this thread, I would like to point out that this type of construction may cause abuse in connection with the open catalogue of risks of the child's well-being by the use of the expression in particular. Second, in my view, it requires what form of foster care such a young man could be covered. My proposal to take care of the matter lies in the gradation of forms of foster care, starting with the most family-friendly ones, i.e. foster family, then a family home of a child, ending up in nursing homes. In the event of an emergency which may arise in connection with the implementation of the provisions of the paragraph, it appears to be possible. 1 the proposed change, such a course of action is right, and the home of the child should be the last resort. The same is true of the case in the mouth. 2 in a situation where parents want to give the child away themselves. The standard should provide for the priority of placing the child in the most family forms of foster care. Next, I would like to draw attention to the problems of interpretation with no definition of the legal concept of poverty. Against this background the following dilemmas appear. First of all, how do you understand the term "poverty"? Second, is this the right criterion for determining the content of the standard? The answer to both questions is negative. For the right determination, the term "minimum existence" or "social minimum" should be used as measurable indicators. As an example of a lack of identification and interpretation problems, one can point out the very justification for the project in which the applicant uses data from the Warsaw and Warsaw-Praska appeals for the period from 1 November 2013 to 31 August 2014, from which we learn about the basis of the application of the foster care measure and from which in 2 cases the sole reason for placing children outside the family was economic considerations, in 37 cases the sole reason for placing children outside the family was so-called. In 20 cases, the sole reason for placing children outside the family was the economic and inefficiencies of the parents' lives. The analysis of cases reported to the Support Phone makes it possible to draw conclusions on the most common causes of rash, unjustified withdrawal of children to parents and the main systemic problems that arise from these practices. About 70% Parents who go to the foundation are parents who are faced with actions to limit or take away parental rights for economic and social reasons, most of whom are: low material status, poverty of the family - the charge against these parents is that they do not meet the needs of their children - poor housing conditions, i.e. too little space or low standard, sudden loss of residence, parental unemployment, job loss, social exclusion, lack of access to institutional support, information and services. All this makes it necessary, ladies and gentlemen, for the criterion of poverty to be fundamentally remodeled into a measuring measure and objectively recognised as normative in the context of the paragraph. 1 standard of Article. 112. ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u438	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! When presenting the draft law on the Bank Guarantee Fund, the deposit guarantee scheme and the forced restructuring, I would like to briefly present the context of the proposed solutions, and then point out the most important changes that this proposal brings to the existing legal order. The financial crisis, which started in 2007, showed that, in the absence of effective instruments at both EU and national level to deal with bankruptcies or failing financial institutions, it was necessary to involve funds from Member States' budgets. It is therefore necessary to have an appropriate legal framework for the compulsory restructuring of financial institutions, allowing for the rapid restructuring of the person in difficulty regardless of its size and market links. Financial stability and the protection of depositors are values that must be protected in the public interest. It is appropriate to introduce such provisions as will reduce the risk of financial stability in the event of deterioration of the economic situation of financial sector entities. The proposed law aims to implement two directives, i.e. the BRRD and DGS Directives. The aim of the BRRD is to establish a system in the Member States where the possibility of involving taxpayers' money in rescuing the banking sector will be significantly reduced. In the case of problems in the banking sector, creditors and bank shareholders are to be the first to suffer losses. The aim of the DGS Directive is to strengthen the stability of the banking system and to increase the protection of depositors, inter alia. by eliminating certain differences in Member States' legislation with regard to the rules on the functioning of the deposit guarantee scheme. What are the relevant solutions adopted in the project? The proposed law provides that the authority for compulsory restructuring, the so-called authority. resolution, in Poland there will be a Bank Guarantee Fund. This fund will be responsible for raising funds for compulsory restructuring funds, in addition to the proposed guarantee funds modelled on existing BFG own funds dedicated to the financing of compulsory restructuring tasks. Therefore, the proposed law significantly extends and increases the importance of BFG, which so far focused only on supporting the recovery processes carried out in banks threatened with bankruptcy. The project provides a legal framework enabling the Bank's Guarantee Fund to carry out compulsory restructuring proceedings, equips the Fund with the necessary powers and restructuring instruments, such as the sale of a banking company, a bridge institution, redemption or conversion of liabilities and the separation of assets, and determines the sources of funding for these processes. The project regulates in detail the entire course of the forced restructuring process, including the principles of cooperation between the resolution authority, i.e. BFG, the supervisory authority - KNF and other institutions in the financial security network, i.e. the Ministry of Finance and the National Bank of Poland, and the resolution authorities from other Member States in the framework of proceedings with regard to banking groups. The proposed provisions provide for a significant extension of the existing Banking Competences of the Guarantee Fund and the KNF. They will relate in particular to the process of preparing the compulsory restructuring procedure, its conduct and any accompanying elements in this respect, such as, first, the creation by the BFG of compulsory restructuring plans, the assessment of their feasibility and the issuing of recommendations to the financial institutions to remove the obstacles identified to the effective implementation of the restructuring procedure, second, the obligation on financial institutions to provide the information necessary for the preparation of forced restructuring proceedings, thirdly, the cancellation or conversion of capital instruments by the BFG, fourthly the finding by the KNF of indications that the entity is likely to fail, fifthly the decision of the BFG to initiate and carry out the restructuring proceedings. The last element is the obligation for financial institutions to maintain an adequate level of liabilities subject to redemption or conversion to the extent necessary to cover losses and restore own funds to at least a level that allows the conditions for continuing operations to be met. As regards the deposit guarantee scheme, the most important changes to the functioning of the system introduced by this Act and the resulting DGS Directive will concern inter alia. shortening the deadline for the payment of funds to depositors from 20 working days to 7 working days, harmonising the scope of the guarantee, i.e. Clarifications of the categories of cash or depositor's claims are covered by guarantee protection, protection for clients of developers' companies, i.e. depositors - entitled to guarantee protection in this case will not only be the developer, as is the case today, but also his customers making payments to the trust account and the guarantee limit will be equivalent in PLN 100,000. Euros. It is also a question of the possibility of temporarily covering certain categories of cash or depositor's claims as defined in the proposed law with a guarantee above the equivalent of 100,000. the euro in gold and the possibility of making cross-border payments of funds guaranteed to depositors, which is intended to increase the level of protection of depositors. According to the draft law, national banks, branches of foreign banks and cooperative credit and savings banks will contribute to the financing of the deposit guarantee scheme and to the financing of compulsory restructuring. In addition, investment firms will also contribute to the financing of forced restructuring. The amount of contributions will depend on the speed of the investigation to the minimum and target levels of the bank and bank guarantee scheme - 0.8% and 2.8 measures guaranteed respectively and measures to finance the forced restructuring of banks and investment firms and the cash register of guaranteed funds. High Izbo! In the opinion of the government, the adoption of the proposed law should increase the security of the banking system in Poland. In the long term, the proposed solution will increase the security and stability of the financial system, thereby reducing the risk of public funds being involved in rescuing those at risk of bankruptcy in the future. Therefore, on behalf of the government of the Republic of Poland, I ask the High House to support this project. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u439	Thank you very much, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear ten-minute statements on behalf of the clubs. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Wiesław Janczyk, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u440	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour on behalf of the club Law and Justice to present the club's opinion on the draft law on the Bank Guarantee Fund, the deposit guarantee scheme and the compulsory restructuring contained in the Seym printing No 215. According to the European Commission's reports, the value of public support to financial institutions only in the countries of the European Union in 2008 and 2012 amounted to more than EUR 2 trillion. Despite such a significant commitment, the threat to financial stability of individual countries and global markets has not been definitively eliminated. In view of the significant aid to the sector in recent years, there has been a belief that institutions that are so large that they cannot fall because their bankruptcy would have far worse and unpredictable effects, hence many participants in the trading and exchange of assets for speculative, investment purposes or those seeking simply to locate their savings remain convinced that countries cannot afford to bankrupt banking institutions, especially large according to standard rules. This further convinces clients of financial transactions with an all-time low level of risk and extends the lack of discipline and criticism, and often lack of common sense when assessing the underlying risks of unreliable counterparties or poorly constructed offers. There is also a belief about the ubiquitous supervision of authorised institutions, which is responsible for everything that is to be bought in the financial services market. Consequently, no one even wonders what he buys in the bank window by order of an institution employee who often cares more about earning a bonus or maintaining a job than on the funds entrusted by the customer. This has a number of consequences. The contested Act on the Bank Guarantee Fund includes one of them, of course the least expected, least desirable, but one which must be available, catalogued, formally described and prepared, even if never to occur. It is necessary to have an appropriate legal framework for the compulsory restructuring of financial institutions to enable the person in difficulty to be quickly repaired, irrespective of its size and market links. Financial stability and protection of depositors are values that must be protected in the public interest. Provision should be made to reduce the risk of financial stability in the event of a deterioration in the economic situation of financial sector entities. In countries such as the United States, Canada, Japan, Brazil, Mexico, Russia, Turkey and the Asian countries, a separate law has been created governing the compulsory restructuring of financial institutions carried out in out-of-court proceedings by the bodies appointed to do so. Not everyone remembers today that in Poland also after the start of the crisis in 2008 the Sejm granted over-standard powers to the Minister of Finance, which was at the time Jan Vincent-Rostowski, to provide support with a one-man decision up to PLN 50 billion from public funds without anybody's binding opinion except himself. A long-standing discussion on whether or not this taxpayer's money could also be remitted on a one-man basis, and ultimately the minister's privilege was withdrawn, but was designed by the government and signed in a Seym version by Prime Minister Donald Tusk. Work on forced restructuring of financial institutions, in particular banks and investment firms, is carried out on a global and European scale. The standardised legislative arrangements developed by the Financial Stability Board, bringing together the representatives of the most developed countries, including the G20 countries, are to be implemented in all countries of the group, also as a result of recommendations from the International Monetary Fund, the World Bank or the Bank of International Settlements. On the basis of the assumptions made in previous working documents, the European Commission has drawn up a draft directive laying down the definitions of corrective actions and restructuring in respect of credit institutions and investment firms. The Directive was adopted by the European Parliament and published in the Official Journal of the European Union on 12 June 2014 as Directive 2014/59/EU, more known as the BRRD or the BRR Directive. It requires Member States to implement in their national legal systems the powers to conduct compulsory restructuring proceedings by means of instruments: acquisition of an undertaking, a bridge institution, separation of assets or redemption or conversion of debt. The Directive, followed by the proposed draft law on BFG, introduces provisions conferring powers on the competent authorities to redeem capital instruments in the context of the restructuring procedure or in the pre-trial stage, and introduces partial harmonisation in order to satisfy claims in insolvency proceedings by giving preference to selected categories of liabilities, in particular to guaranteed deposits. Member States should transpose the provisions of the BRR Directive into national legal orders before 1 January 2015, subject to the provisions on the debt write-off or conversion instrument that may be introduced a year later. In view of the need to implement the draft law into the national legal order of the BRR Directive, it has been adapted accordingly. The entity's scope has been extended to the original assumptions presented by the Financial Stability Committee Working Group with investment firms, financial institutions and holding companies that are parent companies in relation to groups that include an entity in difficulty. In addition, the projector covered the scope of the compulsory restructuring proceedings also cooperative savings and credit banks. In addition, a new Directive 2014/49/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council on deposit guarantee schemes, DGSD, was adopted in June 2014. It took into account the need for harmonisation at European level, inter alia. as regards: mechanisms for financing deposit guarantee schemes and setting the target level of funds collected in the deposit guarantee scheme, determining the basis for calculating compulsory contributions to deposit guarantee schemes, product categories and groups of depositors covered by guarantees, the deadline for the payment of guaranteed funds - a reduction to 7 working days of that period - the possibility of using the funds of deposit guarantee schemes for resolution purposes. The relevant obligations in this respect are set out in the BRR Directive. In view of the above, it turns out that significant changes in the rules governing the functioning of the Polish deposit guarantee scheme are necessary at national level, i.e. in the Bank Guarantee Fund Act. The deadline for implementing a significant part of the provisions contained in DGSD was set by 3 July 2015. At the same time, however, the Directive identifies a number of transitional and adjustment periods for example. gradually shortening the maturity of deposits until the target date of 7 working days - from 1 January 2024. - or to reach the required amount of available funds of the Deposit Guarantee Scheme by 3 July 2024. In view of the nature of the proposed changes and their wide scope, and taking into account the need to organise the existing provisions supplemented by new solutions and thus to ensure transparency, to implement the provisions of the BRRD and DGSD, it was decided to make a new legislative act governing the functioning of the Banking Guarantee Fund, the deposit guarantee scheme and the compulsory restructuring procedure, and not to amend the current Act of 14 December 1994 on the Banking Guarantee Fund and certain other laws. The aim of the proposed Act on the Bank Guarantee Fund is also to modify the provisions concerning the current deposit guarantee scheme in Poland and to create a legal framework enabling the compulsory restructuring of entities falling within the scope of the regulation. In view of the changes introduced by the proposed law aimed at implementing the BRRD and DGSD fund, which has until now been the main administrator of the Polish deposit guarantee system, under new conditions will gain additional powers of the authority for the forced restructuring of banks, branches of foreign banks, investment firms and cooperative savings and credit banks. The proposed law will affect national banks, branches of foreign banks, investment firms, cooperative credit and savings banks, the Minister responsible for financial institutions, the National Bank of Poland, the Financial Supervision Commission, the Bank Guarantee Fund, the Financial Stability Committee and owners, clients, depositors, investors and other creditors. the institution, as well as the Treasury. The aim is to increase the security of financial institutions and their clients. The stability and security of the financial sector providing credit, loan, deposit-taking services of the public and economic operators providing clearing services directly reflects the stable environment in which the institutions and generating companies operate [[♪ The bell ♪]] jobs and economic growth. On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I request that the project be directed to further work in the Public Finance Committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Such action shall be taken in the public interest. The main objective of resolution is to eliminate the negative effects of liquidation under insolvency procedure. The resolution shall start if, despite the implementation of previous supervisory corrective actions, as part of the corrective actions ordered by the Financial Supervision Commission to the entity, the situation is not improved and therefore the continuation of the entity's activities is threatened or necessary, as I have already said, in the public interest, or there is no indication that these corrective actions will soon remove the risk of continuing operations. In the event of a threat to the activities of the entities, the KNF shall inform the BFG, the NBP, the Minister of Finance, the Chairperson of the PSC and other supervisory and resolution authorities. If these conditions are met, the resolution authority decides to initiate these actions and chooses a tool or tools - you have already mentioned them. ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u446	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The proposed draft act on the Bank Guarantee Fund, the deposit guarantee scheme and the compulsory restructuring with draft implementing acts is intended mainly to implement European Union law. Importantly, the draft law was consulted widely with organisations representing the banking sector and the financial services environment at the same time as the inter-ministerial arrangements. The Modern Posel Club agrees with the need to implement the relevant directives of the European Parliament and of the Council and to organise legislation on the Banking Guarantee Fund on this occasion. In our view, the project was prepared fairly, but it is worth noting that the proposed law, which has 360 articles, is far from being recognised as the longest law in the Polish Civil Procedure Code legislation, which, I will remind you, had 1217 articles in its original version, but the draft law, together with the justification, impact assessment and convergence table, covers 1272 pages. With this material size and assumed average reading speed, which is 250 words per minute, it takes about 15 hours to read the whole. Elliot once said, "I would write a shorter letter if I had more time." The time was enough, because since 2014. However, moving to substance - the financial crisis, which started in 2007, showed that, in the absence of effective instruments for dealing with bankruptcies or failing financial institutions, it is necessary to involve funds from the state budget. The European Commission has therefore developed a Directive establishing a framework for corrective action and restructuring in respect of credit institutions and investment firms, which, as I mentioned, was published in the European Union Laws Journal in 2014. The Directive requires Member States to implement in their national legal systems the powers of authorities to conduct compulsory restructuring proceedings through the use of the instruments that Minister Leszek Skiba told us about. In view of the above, it is necessary, on national grounds, to make significant changes to the rules governing the functioning of the Polish deposit guarantee scheme. The purpose of the draft Act on the Bank Guarantee Fund is to modify the provisions concerning the current deposit guarantee scheme in Poland and to create a legal framework enabling the compulsory restructuring of entities falling within the scope of the regulation by issuing a legal act regulating the functioning of the Banking Guarantee Fund. The activities of financial entities are mainly based on trust, as I think we all know here. Losing this trust can lead to panic, resulting in the loss of liquidity of banks and thus the inaccessibility of depositors' funds. Hence the positive directional assessment of the proposed legislation by our club. However, we would like to have the opportunity to continue to work on the project, because many of them addressed by the institutions with which the projects were consulted, e.g. The association of Polish Banks, KNF, was not taken into account. Among other things, the Polish Bank Association paid attention to the costs of the proposed solutions affecting directly the banks' economic capacity to finance the Polish economy, as well as their competitiveness in the single market for financial services in the European Union. In conclusion, the Members' Club Modern is in favour of referring the bill to further work in committee. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u449	Thank you very much, Member. I will ask Mrs Genowefa Tokarska to speak on behalf of the Polish People's Party Club. There you go.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u450	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I will present my position to the government draft law on the Bank Guarantee Fund, the deposit guarantee system and the forced restructuring together with draft implementing acts. The draft law, as has already been said, is the implementation of EU law resulting from two directives: a directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on the recovery and resolution of credit institutions and investment firms, i.e. the so-called. the BRRD Directive, and the April 2014 Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on deposit guarantee schemes, i.e. DGSD Directive. The aim of the BRRD is to establish a system in the Member States which will reduce taxpayers' participation in the event of a crisis in the banking sector. First of all, the losses will be borne by creditors and bank shareholders. On the other hand, the aim of the DGSD is to increase security, to protect deposits by harmonising the rules in this area in all the Member States of the European Union. The draft law from Seym Printing No 215 is a very extensive, extremely detailed document. For many years we have been discussing cutting red tape, and I have the impression that this time government representatives preparing this document have simply forgotten about this basic principle. The Bank Guarantee Fund is an important institution in the financial system, tested under our Polish conditions, which has been operating for many years and has successfully carried out the tasks which it has been established in our country. The fact is that the directives I have already mentioned impose much greater obligations on the BFG, but I am still not convinced that the task of only one BFG requires 360 articles on 280 pages of the document. However, the generally submitted government project is a document being prepared, as you can see, without undue haste, consulted with the institutions to which it will influence, and some of them, such as the Financial Supervision Commission or the Polish Bank Association, expected further details of the records of the standard tasks and submitted them. The tasks arising from the BRRD and DGSD Directives will be carried out by the Bank Guarantee Fund. It is the BFG that will take action to promote the stability of the national financial system, in particular by ensuring the functioning of the mandatory deposit guarantee scheme and the conduct of compulsory restructuring proceedings. The draft law sets out in detail the status, tasks and bodies of the fund. These tasks include the guarantee of deposits, the control of the entities covered by the guarantee scheme, the collection and analysis of information on entities covered by the guarantee scheme or, finally, the conduct of compulsory restructuring proceedings, the preparation, updating and assessment of the feasibility of compulsory restructuring plans. Pursuant to the BRRD Directive, the Bank Guarantee Fund acquires the power to conduct compulsory restructuring proceedings through the use of instruments such as the acquisition of an undertaking, a bridge institution, the separation of assets, the redemption or the conversion of debt. The project regulates in detail the process of forced restructuring, including the principles of cooperation of the body, i.e. BFG, with the supervisory authority, with KNF, with the other institutions of the financial security network, i.e. the Ministry of Finance and the National Bank of Poland, as well as the authorities of forced restructuring from other countries in the context of the conduct of this procedure in the case of banking groups. The proposed solution to give the BFG as a compulsory restructuring authority the power to issue administrative decisions deserves attention, but these decisions will be final and subject to immediate implementation to ensure the effectiveness and speed of the action taken. In the course of the consultation, certain institutions drew attention to the management nature of these decisions, since the administrative court will not be able to annul or annul the decision to initiate the compulsory restructuring procedure, the lack of adequate protection of the rights of shareholders and creditors, in particular in the event of a compulsory conversion or remission of liabilities. High Izbo! This extended security system clearly requires the expansion of BFG institutions and the provision of funding for the implementation of the planned tasks. The mandatory deposit guarantee scheme will be financed by banks, branches of foreign banks and cash from their contributions. The minimum level of funds of the DGS is 0.8% the amount of guaranteed measures and the target planned to be achieved by 3 July 2024 is 2,8% the amounts of the guaranteed measures. The amount of the contributions to that guarantee fund and the methods for determining those contributions shall be determined by the Bank Guarantee Fund and approved by the Commission for Financial Supervision, taking into account the risk profile of the bank or the cashier, membership of the institutional protection system, solvency ratio and asset quality. A separate BFG fund will be a compulsory restructuring fund with a minimum level of 1% for banks, banks and investment firms. the amounts guaranteed and the target is 1,4% of the amount guaranteed. The determination of compulsory restructuring contributions will be based on the value of liabilities less the amount of own funds and guaranteed funds. The determination of contributions and the identification of risk profiles to which entities will be required in both cases is very complex, complex and unfortunately imposed directly by Regulation of the Commission of the European Union 2015/63. The draft law also provides for the introduction of the so-called compulsory restructuring fund. exceptional contributions not exceeding three times the high total amount of contributions paid for the current year. Thus, the costs for the institutions concerned by the regulation of this law are undeniably high. In summary, this project is a very comprehensive, detailed and costly document, and as we know, it is not the only burden on banks. We are also aware that it is fulfilling European Union law, indeed introducing deposit security. This law will be harmonised for all Member States and the club of the Polish People's Party is in favour of working on this document in the Parliamentary Committee. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

The colonel's information, however, was extremely detailed and enabled us to take preventive measures, which offset the Soviet advantage and removed the threat of causing war by them. However, if Moscow had started a war with NATO states, the commander of the Soviet forces attacking Europe, Marshal Kulikov, would have been disarmed with the entire staff no later than 3 hours after the beginning of aggression. Such defence measures could be taken by the United States based only on information previously provided by Kuklinski. CIA Director Clinton and Bush spoke of Colonel Kuklinski during the presidency: This courageous Pole helped to prevent the Cold War from turning into a hot war. He did so, guided by the noblest reason to support the sacred cause of freedom and peace in his home country and throughout the world. Thanks to the courage and dedication of Kuklinski's colonel, his homeland, Poland and the enslaved countries of Central Europe, Eastern Europe and the former Soviet Union regained their freedom. Nevertheless, the court of the Polish People's Republic in 1984 issued a death sentence on Kuklinski's colonel. It was not until 1995 that scandalous sentence was lifted. Colonel Ryszard Kukliński died on 11 February 2004 in Tampa, Florida, until the end of his dream that he would come to Poland again. His ashes were brought to Poland, placed in the Alley of the Decent Military Cemetery in Powązki in Warsaw. By the way - just tomorrow - February 11th, it is worth remembering the words of Colonel Ryszard Kuklinski, our hero: For what I did, I paid the highest price a man can pay - not only with the loss of my sons, but also with injustice and hurtful assessments in my country. They hurt me so much. But I never had a doubt that I chose well. And if I had to live a second time, I would have done the same thing. Everything I've done in my life, I've done for Poland. That's what Colonel Richard Kuklinski said. Hello and praise the hero of Colonel Richard Kuklinski. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u468	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The 76th anniversary of the first export of citizens of the Republic of Poland to Siberia is held on 10 February. Eastern Poland, including Białystok land, also paid tribute to the dead in Siberia today and to those who survived and returned to their homeland. The first export to Siberia, in 1940, was the most tragic and covered around 140 thousand. People. The export took place at night from 9 February to 10 February 1940. At that time mostly military settlers, foresters, inhabitants of small towns, including many peasants and officials were deported. Many did not survive the journey, and many did not return from the scene. Several hundred thousand Poles were exported to Inhuman soil because at the time of the aggression of the Soviet Union they were citizens of the Republic of Poland on 17 September 1939. Deported February 10, 1940 Poles were relocated to Krasnojarskie, Komi. A part of the Polish population was then sent to the Archangel, Sverdłowski and Irkutsk region. Another wave of deportation took place in April 1940 and concerned mainly Polish elites, intelligences, including judges, officials, teachers, as well as families of people deported on 10 February 1940. Kazakhstan was the main place to deflect them. In June 1940, exports to Siberia included in particular Poles and Polish Jews who, after 1 September 1939, found themselves in the Eastern Kres. They were transported mainly to the Archangel region and to the Autonomous Republic of Komi, as well as east of Urals - to the Altai Country, Krasnojarskie Country, Yakutia. In May 1941, the Soviet authorities adopted a decision on further deportations of the population of the eastern lands of the Republic. On 22 May 1941, another mass deportation began, this time mainly the inhabitants of the Eastern Małopolska, the Polish south-eastern border. Deportations mainly included families of people deported earlier, including in 1940. In turn in the night from 19 to 20 June 1941 a mass export of Poles from the so-called. western Belarus, i.e. mainly from the Białystok voivodship and in part from Polesia. The number of deportees is estimated to be 0,8 and 1 million. Some historians say it was only 300,000. persons. That's a lot of doubt. Poles were transported deep into Russia. However, it should be emphasized that during the first 2 years of the Soviet occupation, 1939-1941, many Poles who never returned to their homeland were sent to Siberia. In Podlasie today is the anniversary of exports to Siberia. The celebrations are attended not only by the authorities, but above all by the sibiracs, i.e. those who successfully returned to their homeland and members of their families. Today, the Siberians honor the memory of those who fell victim to the ruthless policy of the Soviet authorities, the tragedy of this first deportation of 10 February 1940 and subsequent - April and June 1940 and the last one that took place in May 1941. Let us remember and honor the memory of those who fell victim to the ruthless politics of the Soviet authorities, all the dead and those who survived the Golgotha of the East. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u472	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to make a statement in memory of Piotr Bartoszcza's heroic attitude.
