Piotr Bartoszcze was a farmer who owned a medium-sized farm in Sławacinek near Inowrocław. Together with his father Michael and brother Roman, he was co-founder of NSZZ RI.Solidarity Boys and a member of the founding committee of this union. At the unification convention in March 1981 in Poznań he became a member of the National Founding Committee of NSZZ Individual Farmers (Solidarity). Since March he has been a participant in the occupation strike of WK ZSL headquarters in Bydgoszcz, as part of the National Strajkowa Committee and on his behalf on 17 April this year he was one of the signatories of the so-called. Bydgoszcz agreements between farmers' organisations and the government party which ended the occupation strike and led to registration in the following month NSZZ RI He was a member of the national authorities of the union and led its structures in Kujawy western and Pałuki. In wartime with their father and brother, they organized conspiracy activities, issuing and colporting underground writings of life and weapons. From September to November 1982, he was interned with his brother Roman. Piotr Bartoszcze died tragically on 7 February 1984. His body was pressed into a sewer pit in Radłówek's field only on 9 February found Roman's brother. Government spokesman Jerzy Urban claimed that he was killed by an accident, suggesting that he was under the influence of alcohol. The circumstances of this death, numerous and unambiguous evidence, as well as indications indicate that it was a murder. Among other things, a car pushed into the river along with numerous signs of collision with a militia car, tracks of prosecution by two people and struggles for life, the way the body is placed in the well clearly indicates the participation of third parties. .Solidarity led an independent investigation proving beyond any doubt the murder. The authorities of the Polish People's Republic ignored the evidence presented by refusing to conduct an open investigation and to seek direct murderers and their rulers. The evidence collected and delivered was destroyed. The reopened investigation was not able to establish anything new in later years and was reassigned in 1995. Direct criminals and their rulers have not been exposed and punished. However, they failed to cover the complete mystery of this secret murder 32 years ago. Today we cannot be sure that we will see the official disclosure of full truth, legal judgment and justice. All the more reason for us to make sure that Peter Bartoszcze's death is not forgotten is not futile. As a nation, we owe our gratitude to those who gave their own lives for their freedom and independence. Respect for our national and regional traditions of the struggle for freedom, their full knowledge and worthy cultivation are a condition for the wise and full enjoyment of the freedom for which he lived and gave his life also to Piotr Bartoszcze. Only then can the sacrifice of the lives of our historical heroes strengthen us spiritually and motivate us to sacrifice for the common good. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u476	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Colony of Parosla I in the Sarne district, 9 February 1943. Exactly yesterday was the 73rd anniversary of the mass murder of Ukrainian nationalists on the Polish population, inhabitants of the village of Parósa in the former Volynsk voivodeship. Murder on the defenseless was done with knives and axes, killing according to various estimates between 149 and 173 people, including women and children. Only 12 Poles, mostly children, were saved. Some of them remained disabled for the rest of their lives. The village of Parsa has ceased to exist. Although the first mass murder, in which about 50 Poles were victims of Ukrainian nationalists, took place in Obobki in 1942, it is the murder in Paros is considered the beginning of the Volyn crime. Its perpetrators, the Ukrainian Nationalist Organization, the faction of Stepan Bandera, and its armed arm - the Ukrainian Insurgent Army, in their own documents the planned extermination of the Polish population were referred to as anti-Polish action. Since then, the scale and frequency of terror has increased to reach its apogee in July 1943. More than 10,000 were murdered at the time. Poles. On 11 and 12 July UPA launched a coordinated attack on Poles in 150 villages. The fact that people were gathered in churches on Sunday 11 July was used. Mass crimes in Poles were committed in 1865 places in Volyn. Extreme cruelty and torture caused this crime to be referred to as the Volyn slaughter. In this way, anti-Polish ethnic cleansing qualified by the investigators of the Institute of National Remembrance as genocide was carried out. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-10-sejm-11-2.u478	Statement on the 270th anniversary of Tadeusz Kościuszko's birth. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! February 4th this year We celebrated the 270th anniversary of the birth of Tadeusz Kościuszko - the national hero of Poland and the United States. He participated in the American War for Independence and in the Polish national liberation uprisings. Tadeusz Kościuszko was born on 4 February 1746 in Merechowszczyzna. He started his education at the age of 9, joining the Piarist College in Lubieszów, then studying in Warsaw at the Knights' School Cadet Corps. In this school, the aim of which was to prepare officers, light, progressive and good citizens, he shaped his views on life. He also studied fine arts and engineering in France as part of his scholarship. Kościuszko returned to Poland 2 years after the first partition of Poland. Without finding a job in the army, he went overseas to join the U.S. Army, where he got a job as a military engineer. During that time, he built defense reinforcements, chains under Saratoga, as well as the West Point fortress. He was soon appointed Colonel. In the U.S. Army he served under the command of several generals, each task doing with great dedication and repeatedly contributing to spectacular victories. In 1783, he received the rank of Brigadier General and the remuneration in the form of land and prize money. The following year, Tadeusz Kościuszko returned to Poland. Soon he joined military service as a Major General. The Polish-Russian war, in which Kościuszko participated, began following the establishment of the trade union and the entry of the Russian army into Poland. After the accession of King Stanisław August Poniatowski to Targowica Kościuszko, he resigned and left the country. Tadeusz Kościuszko and other emigrants were preparing a strategy for the national uprising. He participated in the preparation of the insurrection and after its outbreak in 1794 took over the leadership as the Supreme Chief of the National Armed Forces. As the leader of the uprising, he declared universal mobilization and enlistment to the insurgent army. He participated in many battles, among which the Battle of Raclawice is best known. In the battle of Maciejowice on October 10, 1794. Kościuszko was wounded and was captured by the Tsar. Soon after that, the insurance went down. After the release by the Tsar Paul I, Romanov Kościuszko stayed in the United States and then in Paris. He spent the last years of his life in Solura, Switzerland, where he died on 15 October 1817. His remains were brought to Poland and deposited in the crypt of St. Leonard Wawel Cathedral. The heart of the hero was placed in a urn at the Royal Castle in Warsaw. Since the death of Tadeusz Kościuszko, Poles have been more and more respectful to this character. He is one of the most important national heroes of Poland and the United States. To commemorate his memory in Krakow, a mound of his name was erected, monuments of Kościuszko can be found in many American cities, on the battlefield of Saratoga and in front of the West Point Military University. Located in Philadelphia, the house where the hero lived houses a dedicated museum. Thank you.
